CHAPTER I

On the Questions put by Janamejaya Regarding Krṣṇa's Incarnation

जनमेज उवाच

वासवेय मुनिश्रेष्ठ सर्वज्ञानिधो5नय ।

प्रशुभिष्ठामर्थे स्वागिरसमाकं कुर्वतर्पनि ॥ १ ॥

Janamejaya said: O Vāsaveya! the Chief amongst the Munis, the Ocean of all knowledge, O the Sinless One! the Lord and Well Wisher of our families, I come to you with a mind to ask you certain questions.

Note: Vāsavi is the name of the mother of Vyāsa.

गुरुसेनसुतः श्रीमानसुदेशः प्रतापयान् ।

श्रुतं मया हरिरयेय पुत्रभावमवाप्तवान् ॥ 2 ॥

देवानामपि पुर्वोर भुप्रान्मा चानककुंद्धिः ।

कारागारे कंधे बब्दः कंस्यस्य धर्मतन्तः ॥ 3 ॥

I heard of yore, but I do not know why was that illustrious son of Sūrasena, that powerful Anakadundubhi, (1) the pious and illustrious Vasudeva, the father of Śrī Krṣna, the incarnation of the God Hari Himself, and who was worshipped even by the Gods, thrown into prison by Karīnsa?

Note: Anakadundubhi is the epithet of Vasudeva, father of Śrī Krṣna, since at Vasudeva's birth, drums called Ānakas and Dundubhis were resounded in the sky.

देवक्या भार्येया सार्थ किमागः कृतावनसौ ।

देवक्या बालप्रदकस्य विनाशछा कृतः पुनः ॥ 4 ॥

तेन कैसेन कस्माढ़े ययातिकुलजेन च ।

कारागरे कर्ज जन्य बासुदेवस्य वै हरेः ॥ 5 ॥

What faults had he and his wife Devaki

committed? Why that Karīsa, the descendant of Yayāti, killed the six infant sons of Devakī? And for what reason did the God Śrī Hari incarnate Himself as the son of Vasudeva in the prison house of Karīsa?

गोकुले च कर्वं नीते भगवान्सत्वां चितः ।

गतो जन्मान्तरं कस्मात्सित्ते निगडे स्थिति ॥ 6 ॥

How was that Lord of the Universe, Śrī Bhagavān, and the Ruler of the Yādava clan, taken to Gokula? Why was He, born of a Kṣattriya family, generally recognised as of the Gopāla (cowherd) clan?

देवक्रीमदुर्वेदो च कृष्णस्यामिततेजसः ।

कर्थ न मोचिती वृद्धौ पितर्ते हरिणासुना ॥ ७ ॥

जगत्कर्तु समर्थेन स्थितेन जनकोदरे ।

प्रात्कनं किं तयोः कर्म दुविंत्रयं महात्माथि: ॥ ८ ॥

Why were His father Vasudeva and mother Devaki thrown into prison? And why did not Śri Krṣna, of indomitable prowess, and capable to create and preserve the worlds, could release instantly, father and mother from their imprisoned state?

जन्म वै वासुदेवस्य यत्रासीतयामतः ।

के ते पुराङ्ठा का बाला या कंसेन विधोधिता ॥ 9 ॥

शिलायानिर्माता योनि जाना ताख्छुजा पुनः ।

गाह्रस्थ्च च हरैशूँहि बहुभायांस्य चान्च ॥ 10 ॥

I cannot conceive that the so-called Fate could have any influence on such high souled persons, the father and mother of the Supreme Being, Śrī Krṣṇa; who were those sons of Vasudeva that were

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

killed by Karīsa? And who was that girl child who when struck by Karīsa on a slab of stone, instantly rose up above the sky, assuming the form of Aṣtabhujā, the eight armed Goddess? O Sinless One! Kindly explain unto me how did Śrī Hari manage to perform the house-holder's duties, when he had married the several wives? and what were those glorious deeds that he did in His this incarnation and how did he finally pass away from his mortal physical coil?

क्रार्याणि त्रन तान्येव देहल्याणं च तस्य वे ।

क्रिवन्दना क्षुतं चतम्बनो मोहस्थतीव मे ॥ ११ ॥

My mind sinks into an ocean of confusion, when I hear of the several things done by Śrī Hari; some times I find the deeds, not capable of beings done by any other than the Supreme Being Himself and sometimes I hear of deeds that can be done by an ordinary man. And, therefore, I cannot decide whether Vāsudeva was the Incarnation of God or an ordinary being. Be pleased to remove these doubts from my mind and describe the life of Vāsudeva in its true light.

वर्तिते वासुदेवस्य त्वामख्याहि यवातयम् ।

ननारायणी देवौ पुराणावृषिस्तमी ॥ १२ ॥

In days of yore, the two sons of Dharma were the two best amongst the Rşis, and were the Devas Nara and Nārāyāna.

धर्मपुत्री महात्मानी तपशेत्रतुस्तमम् ।

यी भूनी बहुवर्षाणि पुणये बदरिकाओंम ॥ १३ ॥

निर्वाही त्रित्तामती नि:सूष्टी जितपद्धुणी ।

विष्णोगौरी जानस्येने तपश्रेतुक्तमम् ॥ १४ ॥

They were very high souled persons and they performed severe austerities for long extending years. They were born as part incarnations of Visnu; and, for the good of the world, did they, in the Badarikśrama, perform penances, controlling their six passions, and free from desires.

त्योरंगशताती हि विज्युक्णी महावलि ।

प्रसिद्धो मुनिभि: प्रोत्नी सर्वेत्रेनैरादिषि: ॥ १ ५ ॥

The all-knowing sages Nārada and others say

that the well known Arjuna and Śrī Krṣṇa of indomitable prowess were the two part incarnations of those ancient Munis Nara and Nārāyaṇa.

विद्यामगरीतौ तो कर्थ दैहान्तरं गतौ ।

ननारावाणदी देह्वी पुनः कृष्णाजुनी कवम् ॥ १ ६ ॥

How came those two Devas Nara and Nārāyaṇa to be born in the two bodies of Krṣna and Arjuna, though they did not relinquish their previous bodies!

यो चक्रतुलपश्ठोयं मुक्लवर्धं मुनिसतमो ।

ती कंयं प्राप्तुदैही प्राप्तवोगो महातपो ॥ 17 ॥

And also when those two Munis had attained liberation, their goal in their Yogas, by performing severe penances, how could they again be born in other bodies!

शूर: स्वधर्मान्थितु देहान्ते क्षवियस्तु स: ।

गुणाचातो मृतो वो वे स गुहे प्राहणी भवेत् ॥ 18 ॥

ब्राह्मणो निः:सृष्टः शान्तो भवेरोगाड्मुपचले ।

विपरीतिमंद भाति नरनारायणे च ते ॥ 19 ॥

If any Śūdra dies performing his own religion, he takes up a Vaiśya body in his next incarnation; if any Vaiśya die so, he takes up a Kṣattriya body and a Kṣattriya when adhering to his own rites and ceremonies, dying takes up a Brāhmaṇa body in his next incarnation. And if a Brāhma in be free desires and resorts to the path of peace, when he dies, he becomes free from incarnations and is saved from this disease of getting into the world.

तपसा शोक्तितानी शिक्षी ती कंचयुत: ।

केन ती कर्मण शान्ती जाती शगेघन वा पुनः ॥ 20 ॥

बाहाणी शक्तियों जाती कारणं तन्मुने वद ।

याद्वानां विनाशँध ब्रह्माणाविति श्रुतः ॥ 21 ॥

Now the reverse seems to take place in the case of Nara nad Nārāyaṇa. In spite of withering up their bodies by hard penances, they took up Kṣattriya bodies. Under what influence of Karma, did they take up birth when they were Yogis? Or might they, the Brāhmins, become Kṣattriyas owing to some curse? Whatever it may be, kindly remove my doubts, explaining to me their causes.

Book IV Chapter I

कृष्यामयि हि गाध्यार्थी: शापेनैव कुलक्षयः ।

प्रधुनहर्ण चैव शंबेरण कर्थ कृतम् ॥ 22 ॥

It is heard that the Yādava clan suffered destruction through the curse of a Brāhmaṇa and in spite of Śrī Kṛṣṇa being the incarnation of the Supreme Being, his family died of the effect of curse from Gāndhārī.

वर्तमाने वासुदेवे देवदेवे जनाईने ।

पुरस्य सूतिकगोहादूरणं चातितुर्धेन् ॥ 2 23 ॥

How was it that Pradyumna was stolen away by Sambara, the lord of the Asuras; and for what purpose when Vāsudeva, the Deva of the Devas was present, how was it that his son was stolen away from the lying-in room? This seems impossible.

द्वाराकादुर्भायश्रै हरिवेशमाहूरत्ययात् ।

न ज्ञातं वायुदेवेन तत्कथं दिव्यचक्षुया ॥ 24 ॥

Why did not Vāsudeva see, with His inner vision, the stealing away of his son from the impregnable walls of His fortress like mansion in Dwārkā? (And could thus have prevented this!)

संदेहोरयं महान्वब्धिसः-सन्देहं कृष्ण प्रभो ।

वत्नल्यो बासुदेवस्य दस्यिष्लुक्तित हातः ॥ 25 ॥

स्वर्गेते देवदेवे तु तत्कर्थं मुनिसत्तभ ।

संग्यो जायते ब्रह्धिश्चांतोलनकारकः ॥ 26 ॥

O Munī! After Vāsudeva had gone to Heaven, his wives were plundered by the dacoits on the way. I am very much in doubt on this point. Also I cannot understand why did this event take place just after His translation to Heaven?

विष्णोर्गः समुद्धतः शौरिभूभाराकृत् ।

सं कबी भयगुरात्यं भयाचयत्बना जनादंः ॥ 27 ॥

द्वारवर्यां गत साधो सर्वेन्यः समुद्धरूपः ।

अवतारो हरेः प्रोक्तो भूभारहरणाय वै ॥ 28 ॥

Again how was Śrī Krṣṇa, who was Viṣṇu's Full incarnation, born into this world to take of vicious load of this earth, and to destroy the evil-doers, terrified and He fled through the fear of Jārāsandha, evacuated His kingdom of Mathurā and went with his armies and friends to Dwārkā?

पाणत्मनां विनाशाय धर्मसंस्थापनाय च ।

तत्कथं बायुर्वेदेन चौरात्ते न विगातिताः ॥ 29 ॥

Vāsudeva came here to destroy the evil miscreants and to conserve the religion; how then did He not, as He was all knowing, previously kill those dacoits, who afterward stole and plundered His wives?

यैहृता वासुदेवस्य पत्थ: संर्तुतिहा श्र: ।

सेनातके किं न विज्ञाता: सर्वज्ञं सत पुन: ॥ 30 ॥

भीमब्रोजणधः कामं भूभारुणे मत: ।

अंचित्रा महात्मानः पाण्ड्वा धर्मतन्तरः ॥ 31 ॥

Was it, that He, being Omniscient did not know those dacoits? He protected the Pāndavas, no doubt, who were high souled, righteous and virtuous; but I cannot understand how did He consider the high souled virtuous persons like Bhīsma, Drona and others as loads of earth and slay them.

कृष्णभक्ताः सदाचारा युधधिषुपुगेनामः ।

ते कृत्वा राजसूयं च यज्ञार्जं चियानतः ॥ 32 ॥

दक्षिणा विधाय दत्ता ब्राह्मणेयोर्जितानतः ।

पांबुज्ञान्तु देवांशा यासुदेवक्षिता मुने ॥ 33 ॥

घोरं दुःखं कव प्राप्ताः कव गतं सुक्तं च तत् ।

किं तत्यापं महारौदं येन ते पीक्षितः सदा ॥ 34 ॥

The devotees of Śri Kṛṣṇa, observing good customs, conducts and practices, Yudhiṣthira and his brothers performed the Rājasūya sacrifice, according to rules, giving various offering to the Brāhmins and depended entirely on Vāsudeva; still, O Muni! they suffered terrible hardships; whither were their virtuous deeds by this time? what horrible sins did they commit that they had to suffer pains and troubles in the assembly.

त्रीपदी च महाभागा वेदीभट्यात्मपृत्रिता ।

रामांगजा च साध्यी च कृष्णभक्तिशुता तथा ॥ 35 ॥

The highly merited Draupadī arose from the midst of sacrificial fire and is born of the part of Lakṣmī, pure and devoted to Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

सा कथे दुःखमतुलं प्राप चोरं पुनः पुनः ।

दुःशासनेन सा केशे गृहीता पीडिता भृणम् ॥ 36 ॥

रजस्वला सभायां तु नीता भीतैकवाससा ।

विश्वानगरे दासी जाता मत्थस्य सा पुनः ॥ 37 ॥

How did such a glorious woman meet with incomparable terrible pains often and often; how could she be caught hold of by her hair on her head by Duhśāsana; and carried to the royal assembly, when she was in her menses and extremely terrified and harassed?

धर्पिता कीच्चेताथ रूटती कुरी यथा ।

हता जयद्रेशेना क्रेद्यानप्रतिद्र: सिद्धता ॥ 38 ॥

मोचिता पाणढ़ेः पड़्धाद्वलविद्धमौतामिभः ।

पूर्वेजन्मकृतं पारं किं तदोन च पीक्षिता: ॥ 39 ॥

How did she come to be a slave of Matsya Rāja in his kingdom Virāt, and though actually crying aloud like a female osprey (eagle) she was highly insulted by Kīcaka! Alas! how could Draupadī be stolen away by Jayadratha, though latterally released by the Pāndavas.? What evil deeds did the Pāndavas commit in their previous births, that they had to befall under so many telling difficulties?

इःखान्येनकान्यापतासे कथयाछ महामते ।

रातमूयं क्रतूवरं कृत्वा ले मम पूर्वतंः ॥ 40 ॥

O high minded Muni! My ancestors performed the Rājasūya sacrifice; and still they fell under so many serious difficulties. Kindly explain the cause of these to me.

युःखं महर्ते प्रापता: पूर्वेजन्मकृतेन वै ।

देवांशानां कर्थे तेषां संग्योउरं महान्द्र सै ॥ ४ १ ॥

If it be urged, that they suffered so many serious calamities, due to their actions in their former lives, that seems impossible. Because they are born of the Devas; thus arises my doubt; be pleased to explain to me.

सदाचारितु कौतोवैभिण्ड्रोणादयो हता: ।

छलेन धतलापार्थ ज्ञानानैर्नशं जगत् ॥ 4 2 ॥

Again how the Pāṇḍavas, the sons of Kuntī, of good conduct, and knowing the illusory nature of the world, why did they, out of pretence kill Bhīsma, Drona and others?

प्रेरिता वासुदेवेन पापे घोरे महात्मना ।

कुलं क्षयितवंतते हरिणा परमात्मना ॥ 4 3 ॥

This appears a riddle to me that these Pāṇḍavas were led to the extermination of their race, being inspired by Hari Vāsudeva, to this horrible act.

वरं भिश्रान्तं साधो नीवारैर्प्रिंतं वरम् ।

योधात्र हत्या लोभेन शिल्लेन जीवनं वरम् ॥ 44 ॥

Rather to live on begging alms and to live on rice, growing wild or without cultivation, or to live as an artisan or artist than to kill the valiant warriors unlawfully in a battle, simply out of voluptuous greed.

विच्छ्छस्लु त्या यंगो रक्षितो मुनिसत्ता ।

समुतया सुतानागु गोलकाछ्छनाशनान् ॥ 4 5 ॥

O Best of the Munis! You have preserved this extirpated race by producing the Goloka sons (i. e. sons born by other persons of women after their husbands are dead) of indomitable prowess.

सोउपेवेल तु कालेन विरातदनयासुतः ।

तापस्वस गले सर्षे न्यस्तबाङ्धधद्धुतम् ॥ 46 ॥

And why did my honoured father, born of Uttara in this respected family, encircle a snake round the neck of a Brāhmin ascetic?

न कोर्पि बाहाणं ईहे क्षयिष्य सुकोद्धवः ।

तापसं योनसंयुक्ते पिया किं तत्कृतं मुने ॥ 47 ॥

एतेर-यैशु सन्देशैर्षिकलं मे मनोधुना ।

सिंधं कुरु पितः साधो सर्वोजीसि दयापिति ॥ 48 ॥

No body, born of a Ksattriya family, shews sings of hatred and jealousy towards a Brāhmīn. Is it that my father shewed such feeling to that ascetic, who took the vow of silence!

इति शीमद्वैभागवो चतुर्वैकवन्ये जनोजवप्रसनो

नाम प्रथमोउधाय: ।। १ ।।

O Best of the munis ! These things and lots of others are troubling my mind with many grave doubts. O merciful saint! You know every thing; be kind enough to quell this the disturbed state of my mind.

Thus ends the First Chapter in the Fourth Book of Śrimaddevibhāgavata Mahā Purāna of 18,000 verses by Maharsī Veda Vyāsa.

Book IV Chapter II

CHAPTER II

On the Supremacy of the Effects of Karma

सूत उवाच

एवं पृथः पुराणजो सत्यवतीसुतः ।

परीक्षितसुतं शान्तं ततो वै जनगेजवम् ॥ १ ॥

उवाच संगशच्छेनु वावयं वाव्यविशपातः ।

Sūta said: The learned Vyāsa, the son of Satyavatī, and the knower of the Purānas, when thus asked by Janamejaya, the son of Pariksit, whose heart had become calm, replied in the following words, capable to remove all his doubts.

व्यास उवाच

राजकिमेत्त्रकल्यं कर्मणो गहना गति: ॥ 2 ॥

दुँत्रया किल देवानां मानवानां च का कथा ।

Vyāsa said: O king! You would better know, that in this Universe the course of Karma is not easily comprehensible; even the Devas are not capable to comprehend the wonderful effects of actions; what to speak of men!

वदा समुवित्ते चैनद्वहाण्डे नियुगणात्मकम् ॥ 3 ॥

कर्मणैव समुवत्ति: सर्वेधां नात्र संशय: ।

When this Universe composed of the three Guṇas arose, it was through Karma, that everything had its origin

अनाविंहिन्ना जीवाः कर्मवीजसमुद्धवः ॥ 4 ॥

नानायोगैनु जायते प्रियते च पुनः पुनः ।

कर्मण रहिते देहरभोगो न कहाचन ॥ 5 ॥

It was the seed of Karma whence the Jivas (the individual embodied souls) arose with neither any beginning nor any end. Those Jivas go on often and often incarnating in numberless varieties of wombs and then go to dissolution. When this Karma ceases, the Jivas then are never to have any more connection with any other body.

शुभागुभैरसा मिश्रीः कर्मिभवैहितं तिवसून।

त्रिविधानि हि तान्याहुर्धोर्धास्तत्वविद्धये चे ।। ६ ।।

The Karmas done by Jivas are of three kinds: auspicious, inauspicious and mixed (partly

auspicious and partly inauspicious); of which the auspicious is the Sättvik Karma, the inauspicious is the Tāmasik Karma and the mixed, is the Rājasic Karma. Thus has been said by the Sages.

संचितानि भविष्यन्ति प्रार्थानि तथा जुप: ।

वर्तमानानि देहेउस्मखेलियं कर्माणं किल ॥ ७ ॥

These three again are subdivided into three. They are Sañcita (accumulated), Bhavisya (impending in future) and Prārabdha (commenced.) All these Karmas are in dwelling always with the body.

बहाद्यीनां च संर्वेयं त्रुतगचं नराधिप ।

सुखं दुःखं जगाम्युवृषीणंकादयस्तथा ॥ 8 ॥

कामाकोधी च लोभशु सर्वे देहगता गुणाः ।

दैवाधीनाशु सर्वेयं प्रभवन्ति नराधिप ॥ 9 ॥

O king! Everybody, even Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Maheśa all are under the influence of this Karma! And they experience pleasure, pain, old age, disease and death, joy and sorrow, lust, anger, greed and other bodily qualities, out of the effects of this Karma, which are call ordinarily Fate.

रागद्धिवधो भावा: स्वर्गर्षि प्रभवति हि ।

देवानां मानवानां च तिराशं च तथा वपः ॥ 10 ॥

विकारा: सर्व एवैते दैहेन सह संगताः ।

पूर्वैतरानुयोगेन सैत्ययोगेन वै पुनः ॥ 11 ॥

Therefore love, hatred and other bodily qualities all predominate equally in all bodies Anger, jealousy, hatred, and other similar qualities arise in the Devas, men, and birds owing to some sort of dislikes on previous occasions; and love, compassion, pity etc. arise out of some sort of likings, existing already.

उत्पत्ति: सर्वर्वेन्तृना विना कर्म न विहते ।

कर्माण भ्रमने सूर्य: शगताकः क्षयरोगान् ॥ १ २ ॥

कपाली च तथा रूदः कर्मपौल न संशयः ।

अनाविदिन्यं चैलकाणं कर्म विहते ॥ १ ३ ॥

O king! No individual can arise without some

sort of action or other. It is through Karma that the Sun traverses in the sky; it is through Karma that the Moon was attacked with consumption disease; and it is through Karma that the Rudra holds the disc of skull bone. This Karma, therefore, has no beginning nor end (till Mokṣa); now that this Karma is the sole cause in the production of this Universe.

तेन्हे शाश्रतं सर्वे जनत्यावरर्जगमम् ।

निवातिनिविचारोरत्र निममना मुग्न: सदा ॥ 14 ॥

न जान्ति किमेत्तैई नियं वाज्ञिनियमेव च ।

मायायां विद्यानायां जगत्रित्यं प्रतीयते ॥ 15 ॥

कार्याधिका: कवं याच्य: कारणे सति सर्वेया ।

माया नित्या कारणं च सर्वेषां सर्वेदा किल ॥ 16 ॥

For this reason, this whole Universe, moveable and immovable, is real; but Munis are deeply absorbed in meditation to ascertain about its reality or unreality. They cannot definitely know it for certain whether this world is real or unreal; for where Māyā is prevalent, the universe is there. Where there is the cause fully existing in all respects, there is no effect, how can we say? The Māyā is eternal and always acts as the Prime Cause of all.

कर्मवीजं ततो नित्यं चित्तनीयं सा बुधैः ।

भ्रमवेल जागतर्वा राजकर्मनियन्त्रितम् ॥ 17 ॥

Therefore, O king! the sages declare that the seed of Karma is eternal. This whole universe changes incessantly, being controlled by this karma.

नान्योतिभू गरजेन्र नानाधर्मयेषु च ।

इच्छया च भवेज्ञनय विण्योरिततेजसः ॥ 18 ॥

O king of kings! They say, it is through the will of Viṣṇu, or unbounded energy and splendour, that all this universe enters, again and again, into all sorts of wombs, whether good or evil.

Now, if the birth of Viṣṇu, of infinite prowess, takes place according to His will, then why is that He travels through many impious births? Why is it that Bhagavān Viṣṇu goes, in different Yugas, to take His births in low, vile origins?

युगयुगेचनेकासु नीचयोगितु लत्कवम् ।

त्यक्त्वा वैकुपतर्पावसंग सुख्योगाननेकश: ॥ 19 ॥

Where is that self dependent man, who, leaving his abode Vaikuntha and all sorts of pleasures and happiness, desires to live in this mortal temple, filled with urine, faeces and other filthy matters.

विष्णुप्रमिद्धिर वासं संसरतः कोप्रभावज्ञति।

पुष्यावचलाता च जलकेंति: सुखासनम् ॥ 20 ॥

No intelligent man will leave comfortable resting places and amorous sports and gathering flowers for the sake of dwelling in this uterus in the womb?

त्यक्त्वा गर्भगृहे वासं कोर्षभावज्ञति बुद्धिमान् ।

तुलिकां मुडुसंयुक्ता दिखा गया विनिर्मितम् ॥ 21 ॥

Who likes to live with his face downwards in the womb, when he can enjoy fine heavenly soft downs, puffed up with cotton or silk.

त्यकत्वाधोगुखवासं च कोर्भिधाव्छिति पिड़तः ।

गीतं नृत्वं च वाढं च नानाभावसमन्वितम् ॥ 2 2 ॥

Who will abandon singing, dancing and music, where all sorts of love feelings are being manifested, and think of coming down to this veritable Hell?

मुत्वा को नरके वासं मनसापि वित्तितेयते ।

सिंधुजोब्दुभावानां रां सत्यत्वा सुदुस्यजम् ॥ 2 3 ॥

Who will abandon the wonderful ambrosial nectar and prosperity given by Lakṣmī, that cannot be easily renounced, and then like to taste this urine faeces.

विष्यमूरसपानं च क इक्छेमातिमाशरः ।

गर्भवासात्परो नास्ति नरको धुवनये ॥ 24 ॥

There is no hell more aggravating in the three worlds than this existence in the wombs. The Munis, afraid of these, perform difficult asceticisms in this wondrous world.

तद्वीताश्र प्रकृर्विति मुग्वो दुस्तं तयः ।

हिला थोगं च राज्यं च बने याति मनस्विनः ॥ 25 ॥

Wise, intelligent persons renounce their kingdoms and enjoyments and resort to forests. Who is there so stupid as to enter willingly in the various wombs?

Book IV Chapter VI

तद्वीतस्तु विम्युतात्मा कस्तं सेवितुमिच्छिति ।

गर्भे तुदन्ति कृमयो जठरागिनस्तपतवधः ॥ 2६ ॥

Worms and insects torment the Jivas in the womb; the digestive fire of the stomach heats it from below, whereas it is always fearfully tied down on all sides by the flesh, enclosing its fat or marrow. O King; Not a trace of happiness is visible there.

वपासेवेधनं कूरं किं सुखं तत्र भुपते ।

वरं कारागृहे वासो बन्यनं निगईवैर्म्य ।। 27 ॥

It is far better to live in a prison house, fettered by hard iron chains. Whereas it is not desirable to live for a moment in the womb.

अत्यमात्रं कृष्णं नैव गर्भावासः स्वचिच्छृषः ।

गर्भावसे महदुदुःखं द्रामासनिवसनम् ॥ 28 ॥

It is very hard and painful to be in the womb for ten months. To come out of the hard and terrible womb is extremely troublesome.

तथा निःसरणे दुःखं योपिन्यन्नेजिद्वारणे ।

बालभावे सदा दुःखं मृकाङ्पाबवंसुतम् ॥ 29 ॥

Jivas get trouble in their childhood; they cannot speak, and they do not know what to say, when they are hungry or thirsty; they depend entirely on others and they are grieved.

श्रुचा वेदनाशकः पतन्त्रोक्ताकातः ।

श्रीधेते र्विदे वाले माता चिन्तुरा तथा ॥ 30 ॥

When the child becomes hungry and cries, the mother becomes anxious. When the child is afflicted with diseases and cries, the mother then knows and administers medicines.

नानाविधानि दुःखानि बालभावे भवन्ति वे ॥ 31 ॥

Thus many troubles arise in childhood. Sages do not therefore find any happiness and do not desire, of their own accord, to come here.

किं सुखं विबुधा युद्धा जन्म बाख्छिन चेच्छया ।

संगामामरैः सार्थे सुखं त्यक्त्वा निरन्तरम् ॥ 32 ॥

O king, no sane man would leave incessant heavenly pleasures and prefer before the Devas to this toilsome and painful state of being born in the womb.

कर्तूमिच्छेच्च को मुंहः श्रमदं सुखनाशनम् ।

सर्वेवैल नृपश्रेष्ठ सर्वे बज्हादयः सुतः ॥ 33 ॥

O king of kings! All the Devas, Brahmā and others have to enjoy full the effects of their Karmas done, whether they are pleasant or painful.

कृतकर्मविषयेनक न्रापनुवन्ति सुखासुरुते ।

अवश्यमेव भोक्त्वयं कृतं कर्म शुभाशुभम् ।

देहवादिन्द्रभिवेदीनिर्यभिश्र नृपोतम ॥ 34 ॥

O best of kings! The fruits of karma must have to be experienced, whether auspicious or inauspicious, be he a Deva, or human being or an animal; any one who has embodied himself in fine or gross bodies!

तपसा दानवनैश्छ मानवश्छेत्रतां वजेत् ।

क्षीणे पुण्येध शक्तोर्धि पतत्येव न संपश्यः ॥ 35 ॥

Human beings, by dint of their practise of penance, religious austerities, alms givings and sacrifices, rise to Indrahood. Indra, in his turn, when the effects of his good actions cease, comes down to inferior births! there is no doubt of it.

रामावतारयोगेन देवा वान्तरां नातः ।

तथा कृष्णसहायार्थ देवा यावतरां नातः ॥ 36 ॥

·In the Rāma incarnation, the Devas had to incarnate themselves as monkeys; and in the Krṣna incarnation, the Devas had to incarnate themselves as human beings, Cowherds (Gopas) and Yādavas. एवं युगे युगे विष्णुर्वतारान्नेकानः ।

क्रौति धर्मेश्वार्य बहाणा प्रेरितो भूगण् ॥ 37 ॥

Thus being urged on by Brahmā, Viṣṇu Bhagavān incarnates Himself many times, yugas after yugas, no preserve the religion.

युन: पुरुरैरेरे नानायोनिषु पार्थिव ।

अवतारा भवेन्य्ये रथचक्रवद्धुतः ॥ 38 ॥

O king of mortals! Thus, like a carwheel, Bhagavān Hari incarnated Himself in various wombs successively in a wonderful manner.

दैत्यानां इननं कर्म कतव्य हरिणा स्ववम् ।

अशोगोगे पृथिव्यां वै कृत्वा जन्म महात्मना ॥ 39 ॥

The destruction of the Daityas was done by Hari in His many secondary incarnations.

तदहं संप्रवक्ष्यामि कृष्णजन्मकक्षां शुभाम् ।

स एव भगवातिश्युग्रवतीगों यदो: कृते ॥ 4० ॥

Now I will narrate to you the auspicious facts of the birth of Kṛṣṇa, Who incarnated Himself in the family of Yadu (Yadu Kula).

कथयस्य मुनेरेगो वसुदेवः प्रतापवान् ।

गोयुक्तिरयवत्राजान्युरीणानुभावतः ॥ 4 1 ॥

O king! The illustrious Vāsudeva, born of the part of the Muni Kaśyapa, had to take his birth again as a human being due to his previous curse and had to maintain his livelihood by tending cows.

कथयस्य च द्रे पल्यौ गाणादत्र महीपते ।

अदिति: सुरसा चैवमासु: पृथिवीपते ॥ 42 ॥

देवकी रोहिणी चोभे भगिन्यो भतरर्भि ।

वकणेने महात्रषाणे दान: कोपादिति श्रुतम् ॥ 43 ॥

O best of kings! And the two wives of Kaśyapa, Aditi and Surasā, had to take their births as the two sisters, and Rohini, on account of the curses cast on them. O Descendant of Bharata! We have thus heard that they were greatly cursed at one time by Varuna, the water deity, who got very much angry. The king said.

राजोवाच

किं कृतं कश्यपेनागो येन शगलो महानुषि: ।

सभावं: स कर्षे जातसद्वद्वर्य महामते ॥ 44 ॥

कर्च च भवावीक्ष्यासुरत जातो5तिगोकले ।

वासी वैकुण्डनिलये रमापितरखणितः ॥ 45 ॥

निदेशालक्य यभावात्वरते प्रभृत्यव्य ।

नारायण: सुरश्रेष्ठो युगादि: सर्वधारकः ॥ 46 ॥

स कधं सदनं त्यक्त्वा कर्मवानिव मानुपे ।

करीति जननं कस्मादर मे संगयो महान् ॥ 47 ॥

What fault was committed by Kaśyapa that he had to take his birth along with his wife as cowherds. And why was it that the Everlasing uninterrupted Ātman Viṣṇu Nārāyaṇa had to take his birth in Gokula, He whose abode is Vaikuntha, who is the Lord of Ramā! who is Bhagavān and the Supreme amongst the gods, who is the upholder of the universe and the yugas! Under Whose order can such a being abandon his abode and take his birth in the world like an ordinary mortal? there is this grave doubt, then, of mine on this point.

प्रायः मानुषदेहं तु कांति च विड्वनम् ।

भावारानाविधांतरत मानुषे दुर्जान्यनि ॥ 48 ॥

कामः क्रोधोपर्शोगकी वरं प्रोतश कर्हिंचित् ।

सुखं दुःखं भयं नृणां दैन्यमाजेवमेव च ॥ 49 ॥

दुकृतं सुकृतं चैव वचनं ह्ननं तथा ।

पोषणं चलनं तापो विमर्शश्व विकलबन् ॥ 50 ॥

लोषो दंभस्तथा मोहः कपटः गोचनं तथा ।

एते चान्ये तथा भावा मानुष्ये सभ्यवन्ति हि ॥ 51 ॥

Obtaining this depraved human coil, one is always perplexed with various thoughts, sometimes with lust, anger, jealousy, intoleration, sorrow enmity sometimes with pleasurable feelings, happiness, fear, sufferings, penury, sometimes with straight-forwardness, good or bad deeds, faithfulness, treachery, unsteadiness, supporting others; sometimes with remorse, hesitation, bragging, greed, vain boasting, delusion, or hypocrisy and sometimes with remorse; these different feelings exist in men.

स कर्ष भाषाविण्युलसक्वा सुखमवश्यम्।

करीति मानुर्ष जन्म भावैस्तैस्तैरिधुतम् ॥ 52 ॥

How then can Viṣṇu Bhagavān abandon His eternal pleasures and have recourse to this human birth, full of many perplexing thoughts.

कि सुखं मानुर्ष प्रायः भृति जन मृनीश्र ।

कि निमितं हरि: साक्षतप्रभवां कांति है ॥ 53 ॥

O best of Munis! What peculiar happiness is there in the pleasures of human births, that Śrī Bhagavān Hari has to undertake the burden of dwelling thus in the human wombs?

गर्भेयुःखं जमन्दुःखं बालपाटे तथा पुनः ।

यीवने कामाजं दुःखं गाईल्येउगिमहुत्तम् ॥ 54 ॥

दु:खान्येतान्यवाप्नोति मानुषे द्विजस्तम ।

कर्थं स भावान्विष्युवतानान्यून: पुनः ॥ 55 ॥

O Munindra! The sufferings that are experienced, while in the womb, the pain during the time of delivery, the misfortunes in the early childhood, the troubles of passionate lust in youth, the greater sorrows and difficulties in the householder's life, all these are existent there; how

Book IV Chapter III

then Bhagavān Viṣnu incarnate Himself often in these various human births.

प्राय यमावतार्ति हि हरिणा बह्वायोनिता ।

दुःखं महतरं प्राणं वनवासेस्तिदाक्णो ॥ 56 ॥

सीताविरहर्ने दुःखं संगमश्र पुनः पुनः ।

कान्ताचागो3प्यनैवमनुभृतो महात्मना ॥ 57 ॥

What an amount of enormous difficulties had Brahma-born Hari to undertake in His Rāma incarnation! That high souled One had to suffer for his exile in forest, for the stealing away of his wife Sitā, for the frequent wars, for the final separation from his wife Sitā.

तथा कृष्णावतारोपि जन्म ईशग्रहु पुनः ।

गोकुले गमनं चैव गवां चरणमित्युत ॥ ५८ ॥

कैसय श्नं कष्टद्वाराकागमनं पुनः ।

नानासंसादुःखानि भूक्तवाभ्याकान्धम् ॥ ५९ ॥

Likewise in the Kṛṣṇa Avatāra, the birth in a

prison, the departure to Gokula, tending cows, the killing of Kaṇsa, departure to Dwārkā with great difficulty and all sorts of household difficulties were there. Why had He to suffer all these?

सेवेच्छया कः प्रतीक्षेत चुक्तो कुःखानि ज्ञानवान् ।

संशयं छिपि सर्वत्र मम चित्तप्रजान्तये ॥ 60 ॥

शी शीमद्वीभागवते मातपुराणे चतुर्विक्तचे

घितीयोउपयः ॥ 2 ॥ 11

Who amongst the wise and the emancipated, of his own accord condescends to take on his shoulders so many hard sufferings? This is the grave doubt in my mind; be graciously pleased to remove my this grave doubt and make my mind tranquil.

Here ends the Second Chapter in the Fourth Book of Śrimaddevībhagāvatam of the Mahā Purānam of 18000 verses by Maharsi Veda Vyāsa.

Book IV Chapter III

CHAPTER III

On the Previous Curse of Vasudeva

व्यास उवाच

कारणानि बहून्यायवतते हरे: किल ।

सर्वेयं चैव देवानारंगवतरणोबयि ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! The incarnation of Śrī Hari and the incarnation of the Arniśa Avatāras of all the other. Devas are accountable to many causes. (The chief cause being Karma; the minor cause being many.

वसुदेवावतारस्य कारणं श्रणु तत्वतः ।

देवकमाश्रीव गौहित्या अवतारस्य कारणम् ॥ 2 ॥

Hear, now, the cause of incarnations of Vasudeva (Kṛṣṇa's father), Devaki and Rohinī in detail.

एकदा कश्यपः श्रीमान्यत्राज्यं धेनुमाहरत् ।

याचित्तोयं बहुविधं न ददो धेनुमुत्तमाम् ॥ ३ ॥

Once, on an occasion, Śrīmān Kāśyapa stole away away the Kāmadhenu (the heavenly Cow, yielding all desires) of the Deva Varuna for his sacrificial purpose; and though he was entreated by Varuna often and often to return the cow,

Kaśyapa did not return to him that, the best of all the cows.

वरभान्तु ततो गत्वा बढ़ाणं जगतः प्रधम् ।

प्रणयोवाच दीनात्मा स्वदुःखं विनयान्वितः ॥ 4 ॥

Varuna became very sorry; he went to Brahmā, the Lord of the creation and told him humbly all that had happened and about his sorrows.

कि कंपीम माहाभाग मनोउसी न द्वताति गाम् ।

ग्राणो मया विमृष्ठोउस्मै गोपालो घव मानुषे ॥ ५ ॥

भार्ये ढे अपि तदेव भवेतां चातितु:खिते ।

ततो वत्सा रूदत्यत्र मगृहीना: सुयु:खिता: ॥ 6 ॥

“O Glorious One! Maharṣi Kaśyapa is now almost infatuated with his sacrifice; and though I have tried all my means, he is not returning me my cow. I could not hear the pitiful cries and wailings of the calves on bereavement from their mother; and I cursed Kaśyapa saying “You would go down and take birth in the human world as a cow-herd; and your two wives also are to go there as human

mortals, suffering under the greatest difficulties and dangers."

भुतवस्तासप्रतितस्तस्माद्विवधिति धरातले ।

कारागतिनावसा च तेनापि बहुदुः प्रित्ता ॥ 7 ॥

O Brāhmana! On seeing the distressed condition of the calves I cursed Aditi a second time that the would be put to prison, her children would be still born, and she would suffer lots of troubles."

व्यास उचाच

तच्छुंला चचनं यादोनाशस्य पदापुः ।

समाहूय मुनिं तत्र तमुवाच प्रपापतिः ॥ ८ ॥

O Janamejaya! Hearing this, the Lotus-born Brahmā called Kaśyapa before him and asked.

कस्मात्चया महाभाग लोकपालस्य दोषतः ।

हताः पुनर्न दत्ताश किमन्यायं करोषि च ॥ 9 ॥

O Fortunate One! Why have you stolen away all the cows of the Varuna Deva, the Guardian of a quarter of the world? And why have you committed an offence in not returning the cows to Him?

जान्यायं महाभाग परवितापहारणम् ।

कृतवान्कल्यन्यायं सर्वेजोस्ति महामते ॥ 10 ॥

Bhagavan! You are intelligent, you know everything fully; knowing that it is a sin to steal other's property, why have you committed this unlawful act of stealing away the cows.

अही तोभस्य महिमा महतोप्रे न मुच्छित ।

तोभं नरकंद नूनं पायाकरमर्मनम् ॥ १ १ ॥

Oh! What is the wonderful influence of covetuousness! Even those that are great are not free from the clutches of greed. Covetuousness is the source of all sins, is unapproved by the Sages and leads to hell.

क्यपोष्पि न ते ल्यूके समर्थः किं कतोभ्यहम् ।

सर्वदेवाधिकस्तमालोभो वै कलितो मया ॥ १२ ॥

Lo! Maharși Kaśyapa is not able to leave this vicious habit even now; what shall I do? will hence count greed as more powerful than even Fate, the Ruler of all destinies.

ध्यातसे मुनवः शान्ता जितो वैलॉभ एव च ।

वैखानसै: शमपरैः प्रतिग्रहणराछूगईः ॥ 13 ॥

Besseed are those saints that have devoted themselves wholly to the attain ment of peace, who are tranquil-hearted, lead a hermit life and don't ask themselves of any thing from any body. Verily those are blessed.

संसारे बलवाञ्छुतर्णीफोमेध्योङर: सदा ।

कर्यपोर्गि दुगाचर: कृतनसेहो दुर्गतवना ॥ 14 ॥

This covetuousness is a powerful enemy; it is always unholy and odious. See! Its influence has overpowered the Maharşi Kaśyapa and has tied him down to an ordinary affection and has urged him to commit a sinful act.

बह्यापि तं शगाणाय कर्यपं मुनिसतमम् ।

मयांदारक्षणार्थ हि पौरं परमवललभम् ॥ 15 ॥

अंगेन तंचे पुथिख्यां वे प्रायः जन्म यदो: कुले ।

भार्थ्या संयुतस्तत्र गोपागतलं कौरियसि ॥ 16 ॥

Then the Prajāpati Brahmā, to preserve and keep the prestige in the name of Justice and Religion, cursed his own very dear grandson Kaśyapa, the best of the Munis, and said: "Go to the earth in your Aṃśa, and take your birth in the Yadu clan, be united with your wives and work as a Cowherd."

व्यास उवाच

एवं शपतः कथयपोजसी वरूणेन च ब्रहूणा ।

अंशावतरणाथाव भूभारहणाय च ॥ 17 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! Thus was cursed the Maharṣi Kaśyapa by Brahmā and Varuna to come down to the earth as Anśāvatāra to relieve the earth of her burden.

तथा दित्यासदिति: शगता शोकसंतदाया घृष्णम् ।

जाता जाता विनयेशेरनव पुगास्तु सत वे ॥ 18 ॥

Diti, too, becoming grieved much with sorrows, cursed Aditi that seven of her sons would be killed consecutively after their births.

जनमेज्य उवाच

कस्माहित्या च भारिनी शगतेज्ञजनी मुते ।

कारणं वद्र शगपे च शोकस्तु मुनिसनम ॥ 19 ॥

Janamejaya said: O best of Munis! Why was it that Diti so cruelly cursed his sister Aditi, the

Book IV Chapter VI

mother of Indra? Kindly explain to me the cause of this and oblige. I am sorry to hear of this curse.

सूत उवाच

पारतीक्षितेन पृथस्तु व्यास: सत्यवतीभुतः ।

राजानं प्रत्युवाचेदे कारणं सुसमाहितः ॥ 20 ॥

Sūta said: Thus asked by the son of Parksit, Vyāsa, the son of Satyavati, himself replied to the king about their cause in the following words.

व्यास उवाच

राजन्दक्षसुते हे तु द्वितिःधारितिज्ञाने ।

कथयस्य प्रिये भार्ये बथूवतुरुककमे ॥ 21 ॥

Vyāsa said: Dakṣa Prajāpati had two daughters, Diti and Aditi; these two, of high rank, were married to Kāśyapa; and they were his favourites.

अदित्वां मघवा पुत्रो यदतप्रदृत्तीर्वर्गत् ।

तदा तु तातृषं पुंत्रं चकमे दितिरोजसा ॥ 22 ॥

Aditi gave birth to the very powerful Indra, the king of the Devas. Diti, too, asked for a son of the same strength, prowess, and splendour as those of Indra.

विमामहासितापांगी पुत्रं मे देहि मान्दि ।

इन्द्रतुल्यं बलं वीरं धर्मिष्ठी व्रीयंततम् ॥ 23 ॥

Diti, of beautiful dark blue eyes, entreated to her husband and said, "Give me a son, O giver of due respects to every body! who shall be a hero as strong as Indra, religious and of indomitable energy."

तामुवाच मुनि: कान्ते स्वस्था भव मयोदिति ।

त्रान्ते भविता तुभ्यं शतकृतसूम: सुतः ॥ 24 ॥

The Muni said to her: "O Dear! Be peaceful; I advise you to take a vow, practise a rite, and when the period of your practice will be over, you will get a son like Indra."

सा तयेति प्रतिश्रुच चकार ततमुत्तमम् ।

निविष्तं भुनीना नर्भ विश्राणा सुमनोहरम् ॥ 25 ॥

Diti promised to act according to his word and took an oath; and when she practised the vow, Maharṣi Kāśyapa impregnated the seed in her womb, Diti also bore the seed in her womb according to the usual rite.

भूमी चकार शयनं यपोबतपरायणा ।

पशिया धाणायुक्ता बभूव वरबर्धितीः ॥ 26 ॥

The excellent fair complexioned Diti remained sacred, observed all the rules and, deeply intent on her vow, subsisted only on milk and slept on the ground.

एवं जातः सुसंगुणो यदा गर्भांति विश्वेयान् ।

गृष्धागृधानिरीतर्णांति दिति दृष्टवा तु दुःविता ॥ 27 ॥

मघवत्सदूग: पुत्रो भविष्यति महावल: ।

दिव्यास्तदा मम सुततलेजोहीनो भवेकित्व ॥ 28 ॥

Thus when the foetus was fully developed, Diti began to look white and full of splendonr. On seeing her thus, Aditi became anxious and thought if there be born of Diti a son like the powerful Indra, then my son will no doubt be deprived of his brilliance and splendour.

इति चित्तापरा उपमिन्द्रो चोवारा मानिनी ।

श्रुस्तोडा समुन्त्रजो दितिभर्षेडितीर्वीयान् ॥ 29 ॥

The proud Aditi, thinking thus, said to Indra: "O Son! There, in the womb of Diti, is your powerful enemy.

उपायं कुक नाशाय शजोरा विचित्र च ।

उर्वत्तिरेव हंल्या दिव्या भार्येय शोभन ॥ 30 ॥

O Beautiful One! Even now think out how you can kill your enemy. Before the child is born of the womb, try to destroy it.

वीक्ष्य तामसितापर्गी सप्तनीभावमास्थिताम् ।

दुनौति हदये चिता सुखमर्पविनारिणी ॥ 31 ॥

Since the time I have looked on my co-wife Diti, of beautiful eyes and proud, this is the one and only thought that troubles the peace in the innermost of my hearts.

तराजस्थेल संखुच्दो नहो नैव भवेद्रिपुः ।

तस्मादंकृति हन्यादखुद्विमान्तिं किल ॥ 32 ॥

The enemy, if he firmly gets hold like a fully developed consumption, cannot be killed; therefore the intelligent persons should destroy their enemies, when they are in their bud.

लोहांगुकौक्र विश्वो गर्भों वै हृदये मय ।

येन केनायुपायेन पातयावा तघकते ॥ 33 ॥

O Śatakratu! My heart is being pierced wholly by an iron spoke when I see the womb of Diti; kill it by any means you can!

सामदानवलेनापि हिंसनीयस्वल्या सुतः ।

दिव्या भर्षों महापाना मय चेदिछ्छसि पियम् ॥ 34 ॥

O High minded One! If you wish my welfare, then destroy the foetus, in the womb of Diti, by any of the existent means, Sāma, Dāna or strength and thus remove the cause of grief in my heart."

व्यास उवाच

श्रुवा मातृवचः शको विधिंचेय मतसा तः ।

जगाभापरमातुः स समीपममराधिपः ॥ ३ ५ ॥

Vyāsa said: On hearing his mother, Indra, the King of the Immortals, thought over all the means and went then to his step-mother Diti.

ववंदे विन्यात्यादौ दित्या पापमतिनरूप ।

प्रोबाय विनयेनासी ममूरं विशर्पितान् ॥ ३६ ॥

That evil minded Indra bowed down at the foot of Diti with humility and addressed her with words, sweet but full of poison.

इन्ट उवाच

भातस्वे ततयुक्तप्रसि क्षिणद्दोर्जान्तद्वैत ।

संवार्थीमू संप्राप्नः किं कर्तेयं वद्यव मे ॥ 37 ॥

“O mother! You have become very weak, lean and thin in the practise of your vow. I have come to serve you; order me now what I can do for you.

यावसंवाहन्ते तेर्कं कृत्याभ्यम पतितं ।

गृहशूक्ष्णातपुर्णं लभते गतिमक्षायाम ॥ ३ ८ ॥

O chaste one to your husband! I want to shampoo your feet. To serve one's Guru means to earn righteousness and immortality.

न मे किमपि भेदोर्सित तथातिद्या शपे किल ।

इत्युस्चना चरणी स्पूच्या सेवाहनपरो3पवत् ॥ 39 ॥

O mother! I swear, on oath, I don't make any difference between you and my mother Aditi." Saying thus, he touched her feet and began to shampoo her legs.

संवाहनसुखं प्राय निद्रामय सुलोचना ।

शांता तत्कुशा सुमता विश्रता परम सती ॥ 40 ॥

The beautiful eyed Diti, tired of the vow, lean

and thin, thus being shampooed and having full faith in Indra's words, fell to deep sleep.

तां निद्रावरामपातं विलोक्य प्राविशतन्नु।

रूपं कृत्वात्रिसूभं च शरक्षाणिः समाहितः ॥ 41 ॥

उदरं प्रविवेशगृ तत्या योगवलेन वै ।

गर्ष चकर्ते वज्जेण सन्ताया पर्विनायकः ॥ 42 ॥

Seeing her asleep, Indra, with thunderbolt in his hand, took his subtle form and by the influence of his yogic power, entered carefully into her womb quickly and cut asunder the foetus in the womb into seven parts.

रोदे च तदा वाले वज्जेणाशिन्तस्तथा ।

मा रहेति शनैर्वाक्यमुवाच मघवानुम् ॥ 43 ॥

शकलानि पुनः सप्त सप्तथा कतिलानि च ।

तदो वैकॉनंचाशन्मतक्ष्राभववुम् ॥ 44 ॥

The child in the womb, struck by the thunder bolt, cried out. Indra spoke to the child gently: "Do not cry," and in the mean while cut each of the seven parts into seven parts again. Thus, O king! The forty-nine Maruts were born.

तदा प्रबुद्धा सुदर्ती जाला गर्भ तथा कृतम् ।

इन्हेण छलकर्णे चुकांप भुशपु:वित्ता ॥ 45 ॥

When the good natured Diti awoke, she came to know that Indra has treacherously cut the foetus in her womb and became very sorry and angry.

भगीनकृतु तु सा बुद्ध्या शणाग कुपिता तत ।

अदिति मघवन्तं च सच्वततपरायणा ॥ 46 ॥

यथा मे कर्तिते गर्भरतव पुत्रेण छघना ।

तथा तत्रागमापातु राज्यं त्रिशुवनस्य तु ॥ 47 ॥

Knowing that all these treacherous acts are really done under the advice of her sister, the truthful Diti; who was under the vow, cursed Aditi, and Indra, saying that as her son Indra has treacherously cut the foetus in her womb, Indra's kingdom over the three worlds would be destroyed.

यथा गुप्तेन पायेन मम गर्भं निवातितः ।

अदित्या पापचारीपया यथा मे पातितः सुनः ॥ 48 ॥

तस्या: पुजास्तु नस्पन्तु जाता जाता: पुनः पुनः ।

कारागरे वसलैया पुत्रगोकारुप भृगम् ॥ 49 ॥

अन्यज्मनि चायेव मृतापत्या भविष्यति ।

Book IV Chapter IV

And as the sinful Aditi has secretly caused the destruction of my son, her sons, too, would also die after their birth consecutively and she would dwell in the prison house in much trouble and anxiety and would also bear still born sons in her next birth.

यास उत्तरा

इत्युसूई तदा श्रुत्वा शापं मरीचिनन्दन: ॥ 50 ॥

उदार प्रणयोपेता वचनं समयबिल ।

Vyāsa said: O king! Maharṣi Kaśyapa, the son of Marīci, hearing the curse, allayed her anger with loving words.

भा कोर्भ कुरु कल्याणि पुत्रासे बलवतरा: ॥ 51 ॥

भविष्यन्ति सुरा: सर्वे मन्तो मवत्सतखा: ।

O Blessed One! Do not be angry. Your sons would all become very powerful and would be called Maruts. They would be companions and friends to Indra.

शापोयं तव यामोक त्वहार्विंशेध द्वारे ॥ 52 ॥

अंशेन मानुं जन माप थोभ्यति भामिति ।

O Dear! Your curse won't be fruitless; in the 28th Manvantara, at the end of the Dvāpara Yuga, your curse will bear fruit. Then Aditi, sinful for her jealousy and anger, will go down on earth to

take the human birth through her Anīsa (part) and suffer according to your curse.

वक्तोनापि द्नोडसि शाप: सन्तापितेन च ॥ 53 ॥

उभयो: शापयोगेन मानुरीयं भविष्यति ।

Varuna, too, had become very grieved and cursed her. And, due to both these curses, this Aditi will be born as a woman.

व्यास उवाच

पतिनाउंश्रासिता देवी नसूला सोउभवस्त्ता ॥५४।।

नोवाच विधिप्रं किक्षित्ततः सा वरवर्षिणी ।

O King! The fair complexioned Diti, thus consoled by her husband, became glad and did not utter any more unpleasant words.

इति ते कविरा राजन्यरोगायस्य काराणम् ।

अदितिर्देशकी जाता स्वांगेन नृपसत्तम ॥ 55 ॥

O king! Thus I have narrated to you the cause of the previous curse. O best of kings! Thus Aditi was born as Devakī out of her Anísā.

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणे चतुर्विकन्ये

तृतीयोउद्यानः ।। ३ ।।

Here ends the Third Chapter of the Fourth Book of the Mahā Purānam Śrīmaddevibhāgavatam, of 18,000 verses on the former curse of Vasudeva and Devakī by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book IV Chapter IV

CHAPTER IV

On Adharma

राजोवाच

विसितोरभि महाभाग श्रुलारा उख्यानं महामते ।

संसारोउं घापहप: कर्थ मुच्येत बन्यन्ति ॥ १ ॥

The King spoke: O highly honoured and intelligent one! I have heard the anecdote just related to me by you. I am very much bewildered. This Samsāra (world) is vice incarnate. I wonder how the Jivas, entangled in its meshes, can again be freed!

क्रव्यस्यायि द्वादादिस्लोकविधेसे सति ।

कृतवानीदूर्ण कर्म को न कुयोज्वुपिस्तान् ॥ 2 ॥

When the son of Kaśyapa, whose kingdom is the three worlds, can commit such an heinous act,

what wonder, then, that any other ordinary person would do more blameable things!

नार्ध प्रविष्य बालस्य हूननं दाक्णा किल ।

सेवाधिरेण यानुछं कृत्वा शपथम्बुद्धून ॥ ३ ॥

On the pretence of serving and on a solemn oath, when a man can enter into his step-mother's womb and take away the life of the son, what more heinous and event dreadful can take place than this!

शास्ता धर्मेय योगा च जितोकया: पतित्यत् ।

कृतबानीदुर्ग कर्म को न कुर्दादसांग्रिन् ॥ 4 ॥

When the preserver and controller of religion, the ruler of the three worlds can do such acts, you

cannot expect that any other person would desist from committing heinous, contemptible acts.

पितामहा मे संग्रामे कुरूक्षेत्रेस्तदार्णम् ।

कृतवर्तनतावस3शर्षं दूरं कर्म जनतानुगे ॥ 5 ॥

O World Teacher! Indeed my grandfather did unjustifiable horrible mean acts in the battle field of Kuruksetra. It is really wonderful!

भीमो उराण: कुप: काणं धर्माशिप विध्धितः ।

सर्वे विक्बद्धर्मोग वासुदेवेन नोदिताः ॥ 6 ॥

असारातं विजानन्तः संसारस्य सुधेधसः ।

देवांशाशु कर्व चकुनिंन्दितं धर्मतत्पराः ॥ 7 ॥

Bhīsma, Droṇa, Krpa, Kārṇa, even Yudhīsthira, who is the part incarnate of Dharma all these were urged by Vāsudeva into this contrary religious act. These personages are all born of Devānās, devoted to religion, and intelligent. These know the transitory nature of this world; how can these commit such mean blameable things!

कासस्था धार्यव विश्रंत्र प्रमाणं किं विनिश्छिन् ।

चलिण्तोस्मि संजातः शुत्वा चैतकाधानकम् । ८ १ ॥

O Glory of the Brāhmins! What faith or regard can we have for a religion, when such high souled persons commit such irreligious acts! Indeed, there is doubt whether religion exists at all or not! O Best of the Munis! My heart is agitated very much on hearing these incidents.

अनवाक्यं प्रमाणं चेद्यातः कः: परहेद्वान् ।

पुको विश्वासात्तो रागी भवति सर्वेथा ॥ 9 ॥

If it be said that the word of the Āptas (seers) is a sufficient guarantee for the entity of religion, it may then be questioned where there is such an Āpta, holding such a pure religious body? All those persons who are attached to woldliness are bent on all worldly objects with their whole head and heart; these, therefore, cannot be Āptas.

रागो ऋषो भवेघुन्यर्थानागरसंगयम् ।

द्वादस्यवचनं वक्त्वं स्वार्थसिद्धये ॥ 10 ॥

When self interest is obstructed, jealousy and anger arise; and to secure one's self interest, out of jealousy, arise untruthful words.

जरासंधविधातार्थ द्विरिण सत्त्वमूर्तिना ।

छलेत्र तिचेत रूपं ब्राह्मणस्य विज्ञानता ॥ १ ॥ ॥

Even the pure, excellent, virtuous Śri Krṣṇa, with full consciousness, had to assume under pretence a Brāhimn form for killing Jarāsandha.

तद्वतः कः प्रमाणं किं सत्त्व्यूर्तिपौदुगः ।

अज्ञैनोर्षि तर्वेवास कर्यं चूत्रवित्थिनिति ॥ १२ ॥

Just as the holy Śrī Hari assumed a false appearance to kill Jarāsandha, similarly Arjuna, too, did a false sacrifice to accomplish his ends. Where is, then, one who can claim to be an Āpta? And what proof of there is the existence of such an Āpta?

कोदुशोर्धवं कृती वयः किमर्थं शमवर्जितः ।

परलोकपदार्थ वा यशसे वात-न्यथा किल ॥ 13 ॥

What sort of sacrifice was this? Did it lead to heaven in the next world or did it lead to glory or did it serve the cause of any goodwill? Why was it deprived of that peace and rest? (It was performed with a view to kill Śísūpāla and others).

धार्मेय प्रथम: पाद: सत्ययेतकुतेर्षतः ।

द्वितीयस्तु तथा शोचं दया पादसूतीयकः ॥ 14 ॥

दानं पादशुत्थंध्र पुराणजा वदन्ति वै ।

तैर्वितीतः कथं धर्मसिंहिद्र सुमंमतः ॥ 15 ॥

The Pundits, of yore, declare that truth is the first Pāda, cleanliness, the second; compassion, the third; and charity is the fourth Pāda (foot) of Dharma (Religion). Thus, devoid of these, how can Religion stand with due regards from all?

धर्महीन कृतं कर्म कर्ब तत्कलं घरेत् ।

धर्मे स्थिति मत: स्वापि न कस्यापि प्रतीयते ॥१६।१

How can an act bear good fruits, which has no trace of virtue in it? It seems no one had any trace of faith and steadiness in one's religion. (The Pāndavas did sacrifice out of arrogance; how can they be Āptas?)

छलाई च तदा विद्युविमानोतःपूजामतमुषु: ।

येन वामनकोण यंचितो३जी बलिनूप: ॥ 17 ॥

विहर्ता शलयत्रस्य वेद्यानं परिपालकः ।

धर्मिष्ठी दानशील सत्यवादी विलेत्रियः ॥ 18 ॥

Book IV Chapter IV

स्थानात्यप्रशितो5कसामविष्याना प्रभविष्याना । जितं केन तयोः कृष्ण बलिना वामनेन वा ॥ 19 ॥ छलकर्मविद्य चापं सन्देहोउत्र महान्यम् । वश्यित्या वश्चितेन सत्यं वद्य तिततोम् ॥ 20 ॥ पुराणकर्ता त्वमिति धर्मईघ महामिति: ।

Víṣṇu, the Lord of the Universe, assumed His Dwarf (Vāmana) Incarnation with the express object to cheat Vali, the king of the Daityas. Now, O Muni! The king Bali, performed one hundred sacrifice; he was the protector of the Vedas, virtuous, charitable, truthful and self-controlled; why was such a man dislodged from his position by Víṣṇu, the Powerful. Who was victorious in this affair? Was he the Bali, who was cheated? Or was it Vāmana Deva, the expert in making nice pretence? Who was the better of the two? I have got grave doubts on this point. O the best of the twice-born! You are the composer of the Purāṇas, virtuous, and liberal hearted. Speak what is true (and thus tranquil my heart).

व्यास उवाच

वित्त वे बलिना राजन्त्ता येन च मेदिति ॥ 21 ॥

विविक्रमोपि नाना यः प्रवितो वामानोभवत् ।

छलनार्थिमं राजन् वामनत्व नराधिप्य ॥ 22 ॥

संप्राप्त हरिणा भूयो द्वारपालत्वमेव च ।

सत्याद्यन्तराजसित मूलं धर्मेत्य पार्थिंद ॥ 23 ॥

दुःसाध्ये रैहिंना राजनस्वी सर्वसेना किल ।

Vyāsa said: O king! The victory was certainly Bali's in as much as he fulfilled his promise and gave over his kingdom of earth to Viṣṇu. And in as much as Viṣṇu in his fifth or dwarf Incarnation deceived Bali, he had to become a dwarf (i.e. a small mean person indicated even by the shortness of his body). O king! There is nothing superior in religion to truth. See! Śrī Hari even had to become, for his falsehood, a gate keeper of Bali. O king! It is hardly possible for a human being to observe in every way the injunctions of truth.

माया बल्वती घपू विभुगा बहुकशिपी ॥ 24 ॥

ययेदे निर्मिति विधं गुणै: शबलितं त्रिभि: ।

Powerful, indeed, is Māyā, composed of the three qualities and of various forms. By Her is created this Universe, made manifold by the admixture of the three qualities (Sattva, Rajas and Tamas).

तस्माखलतं सर्व कुतोर्शिद्धं भवेयुए १। २५ ॥

मिश्रण जनितश्रेय स्वितिरेया सन्तानी ।

How can you expect therefore, truth to be observed wholly, without the least violation, by a deceiver. This world is made up of the mixture of Rajas; O king! Know this as the every day routine of things in nature.

वैखानसाधा मुनवो नि:संगा निश्रितिधूतः ॥ 26 ॥

सत्ययुक्ता भवन्यत्र वीरातगा गततृषः ।

दूर्धान्दर्जानाथर विनिमिताने च तादूशाः ॥ 27 ॥

अन्यत्सर्वे शबलितं गुणैरिभितिबिंग्रु ।

It is only the Munis and Hermits that can observe pure truth; and that is why they are without any attachment; they do not accept any thing from any other body; they are desireless; and they all have no rough wear and tear of the world. They exist as perfect examples; their case is quite separate. All the others are caught under the meshes of the three Māyīc Guṇas.

नैकं वाक्यं पुराणेषु वेधेयु नृपस्तम १। २८ ॥

धर्मशास्त्रेषु चांगेषु सगुषी रचितेशिक्त ।

O Best of kings! The Dharma Śāstras, Purāṇas and the Angas of the Vedas are full of diverse opinions on any one point under consideration; for their composers were under the influence of the different Gūṇas.

सयुण: सयुणं कुर्बाशिवर्णं न करोति ते ॥ 29 ॥

गुणास्ते सिश्रिता: सर्वे न पृथभावसंगस्ता: ।

The Suguna persons (i.e. persons under Māyā) do Saguna works (works composed of qualities) and the Nirguna persons (i.e. persons above Māyā) do not do any Saguna work. And when the Guna are mixed with one another, they cannot remain pure (i.e. they exhibit qualities of those Gunaş with which they are mixed).

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

निव्यौतिके स्थिते धर्म मति: कस्यापि न स्थिति ॥ 30 ॥

भवो.टूबे महाराज मायया मोहितस्य वै ।

O king! One is influenced by Māyā no sooner one takes one's birth in this world; so that no body can remain steady in this pure, steady maxim of truth, untainted by any falsehood or deceit.

ईन्द्रियाणि प्रमाधीनि तदासर्फे मनसला ॥ 31 ॥

क्रातील विवियाभ्याल्यानुगीत: प्रेरितो भृणम् ।

The sense organs, Indriya, as, confound the Buddhi (reason) and make one follow the path of enjoying sensual things. Mind is attached to senses and follows diverse ways, urged on furiously by the three Gunas.

वशातिवर्षयपत्न : प्राणिनः : स्थितरंगमाः : । ३ २ । ॥

सर्वे मायावगा राजनसाउनुकीक्षिति लेरिह ।

O king! All the beings, Brahmā down to the moving and non-moving things, fall under the delusion of Māyā; She plays with them.

सर्वानैं मोहयत्येया विकुर्वेत्यिनगं जगत् ॥ 33 ॥

असलो जायते राजकार्यवान्यमं नरः ।

This Māyā is always imposing on all; and She is incessantly making formations and transformations in this Universe; O king of king! The man under the influence of action takes recourse to this untruth (i.e. actions arise first from this untruth) from the very moment of his birth.

शून्द्रियार्थीऋतयानो न प्राणानीत यदा नरः ॥ 34 ॥

तदर्थं छलमादने छलायपे प्रवर्ते ।

Persons when they do not get their desired object after they have pondered how to secure the sensual objects, take recourse to pretext, and, from that pretext do many sinful acts.

कामः क्रोधश लोभश्र वैरीणो बलवरतः ॥ 35 ॥

कृताकृतं न जानित प्राणिनस्तशं गता: ।

Lust, anger, and avarice; these are very powerful enemies. The Jivas under their influence cannot distinguish right from wrong.

विघये सत्यहंकार: प्रबलः प्रभावलपि ॥ 36 ॥

अहंकाराद्वेभोहो महामरणाये च ।

When wealth, might and rank come to a person,

he gets deep-rooted Aharinkāra, and becomes very egoistic; from Aharinkāra, delusion comes and, from delusion, insensibility and death ensue.

सकूल्य बहुवस्त्त विकल्पा: प्रपवन्ति च ॥ 37 ॥

ई्योंमृया तथा ईयः प्रायुर्भवित् चेतासि ।

आशा तृष्णा तथा दैन्यं द्यमो5धर्मापीतस्तथा ॥38 ॥

प्राणिज्ञा प्रभवेत्येते भावा महामुद्धावः ।

Here men argue mentally many plans; and thence jealousy, intolerance and enmity spring in the heart; next arise, out of delusion, hope, thirst, misery, low-spiritedness, arrogance and irreligiousness.

यज्ञानानि तीर्थानि ज्ञानानि नियमास्तथा ॥ 39 ॥

अहूब्रात्याभ्यन्तु कगोति पुरुषोउवहम् ।

It is through Ahamkāra that people are led to perform sacrifices charities, visiting places of pilgrimages, practising vows and rules for religious rites and ceremonies.

अहंभावकृतं सर्वे प्रपवेत्रे न शीचवत् ॥ 40 ॥

रागलीभावृक्तं कर्म सर्वांग शुद्धिवर्जितम् ।

Hence these sacrificial acts, etc, from Aharñkāra, are unable to remove the clouds of impurity from the mind, as observance of purity and cleanliness does. Especially when any action is done through greed or undue affection, as its motive, it cannot be pure in every respect.

प्रधानं रद्वयगृद्धिक्र शद्वया विबुधेः किल ॥ 4 1 ॥

अउहोष्णासितं रद्यं प्रगलं धर्मकर्मणि ।

Therefore, at the commencement of any sacrifice, the wise persons looks at the purity of sacrificial things; (Dravya Śuddhi); those articles that are collected without injuring others, are the best in religious acts.

उहार्जितेन रुद्येण यक्योति शुभं नः ॥ 42 ॥

विपरीतं भवेत्तु फलकाले नृपोतम ।

O best of Kings! If the things, acquired by injuring others, be utilised in any auspicious act, they yield contrary results at the time of fruition.

मनोरितिनिरंच यस्य स व्यव्यरूकतापाषयेत् ॥ 43 ॥

तस्मिन्विकारयुक्ते तु न यथार्थपर्ल लभेत् ।

Book IV Chapter V

It is he only, whose mind is very-pure and undefiled, who gets the results wholly auspicious from any sacrificial act. Minds defiled do not acquire their proper desired objects.

कताऱ: कर्मणां सर्वे आचार्यश्रस्तिजाद्यः ॥ 44 ॥

सूभे त्रिशुद्रमनसस्तदा पूर्ण भयेकतमम् ।

देशकालतिकागद्यमकुर्णो शुळता यदि ॥ 4 5 ॥

न-नाणां च तदा पूर्ण कर्माणं फलमपुन्ते ।

When the preceptor and the priests ordained are sincere and pure; more over, when the place, moment, act, sacrificial things, the mantras, and the sacrificer are all holy, then and there only, the full results accrue in their entirety to the sacrificer.

श्रूर्णो नारामहिष्य स्वयुद्धि परभां तथा ॥ 46 ॥

क्रांति सुकृतं तद्धिपरितं भवेकित्कि ।

If the sacrifice be intended for the destruction of one's enemy or for a personal motive and one's gain, it converts auspicious results into those that are inauspicious and leads to ruin in the ends.

स्वाधिसकः पुणप्रियरं न जानाति शुभाशुभम् ॥ 47 ॥

देवाधीन: सदा कुध्यत्पापमेव न सकृतम् ।

Selfish persons are unable to ascertain, which actions are auspicious and which are not; they depend on the circumstances what they call Daiva, and the people do acts sinful instead of virtuous.

प्रजापत्या: सुरा: सर्वे हुसुराधुः तदुद्धना: ॥ 48 ॥

सर्वे ते स्वार्थिनस्ताः परमसरविरोधिनः ।

सचोद्धता: सुरा: सर्वे प्ययुक्ता वेवेयु भागुना: ॥ 49 ॥

रजो-द्वावसातमासासु तिथच: परिकीर्तिता: ।

The Devas and demons all are created by Brahmā, the Prajāpati, the Creator; they all are selfish; hence they are always at war and war with each other. The Devas are born from the Satīva Guna; the human beings are sprung from the Rajas and the birds are sprung from the Tamas.

सत्त्वैद्वानां तैर्वरं परम्यमनातन् ॥ 50 ॥

तिरधान्य कि चित्र जातिवैरमुद्धे ।

O King! When the Devas, born of the Sativa Guna are always engaged in inimical actions, what wonder, then, is there, that the lower ones would be at war with one another!

सदा श्रौहमर देवास्तपोश्चनकारतथा ॥ 51 ॥

असन्तुषा देषपारः परम्यस्तेषिधिनः ।

अहूबारसपूद्रतः संसारोउयं यतो नृप ।

रागदेषविहीनस्तु स कर्व जायते नृप ॥ 52 ॥

शीत श्रीमदीभागवते महापुराणे वतृष्वैकन्ये

बतुर्षो5थायः 11 4 11

O King! When the Devas are always discontented, filled with jealousy and envy, at war amongst each other, and obstructors of the ascetics and the austere persons, then know that this Universe has sprung from Aharñkara (egoism). How can you expect them to be free from feelings of anger, jealousy; etc.!

Here ends the Fourth Chapter of the Fourth Book, the Mahā Purānam Śrimaddevībhāgavatam of 18,000 verses on Adharma by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book IV Chapter V

CHAPTER V

On the Dialogues of Nara Nārāyaṇa

व्यास उवाच

अथ विक यदुनेतेचन संसारोस्मिध्योतम ।

यर्मान्मारोडीहबुद्धितु कक्रिद्धवति कर्हिंधन् ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O best of kings! There is no need of dwelling at length on this point; suffice to say, that in this world, are found persons very rare that are religious, and free egoism, jealousy, anger, etc.

रागदेश्वार्ते विश्वं सर्वे स्थावरजंगम् ।

आष्टे युगेपि राजेन्द्र किमष्ट्र किलदूषिते ॥ २ ॥

O king of kings! Even in the Satya Yuga, the Golden age, this world, moving and unmoving, was covered with feelings of jealousy and anger. What to say in this Kali Yuga (Dark Age)! (There is no wonder that this world would be full of these vicious things.)

देवा: सेव्यांश सहोहाण्डलकर्मरता: सदा ।

मानुपाणां तिरक्षां च का वातां नृप गणयते ॥ 3 ॥

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

O best of kings! When the Devas are deceitful, jealous, and filled with feelings of anger, what is to be said with human beings and other lower creations!

ग्रैहोर प्रेहरो पर्वेदिति समानता ।

अग्रौहिण तथा शान्ते विहेषः खलता स्मृता ॥ 4 ॥

O Lord of the Earth! It is natural, that injury be inflicted on those persons that commit injury; but when peaceful person, void of any enmity, are injured, that is certainly an act wicked and mischievous.

यः कश्रितापसः शान्तो जपध्यानपरायणः ।

भवेतन्य जपे विद्यकर्ता वे मघवा पत् ॥ 5 ॥

Whenever, any devout ascetic, calm and quiet, is engaged in prayer and meditation, and silent muttering of one's martrams, the king of the Immortals throws hindrance in his asceticism. (This is certainly a mischievous act.)

सर्ता सत्यगुं सश्रात्सर्वदैवावर्ता कति: ।

मध्यमो मध्यमानां तु क्रियायोगी युगे स्मृती ॥ 6 ॥

(Holy, unholy and mixed persons exist in all the yugas). To those that are holy, all the yugas are the Satya yuga; to the unholy ones, always it is the Kali yuga (Dark age); and to the mixed ones, always it is Tretā and Dvāpara.

कीक्षित्दाचिद्वलिति सत्यधारुनतैकः ।

अन्यधारुव्ययुगानों से वर्से धर्मपरायण: ॥ 7 ॥

You will very seldom find a few persons, following really the True Religion; otherwise, you would have found all the persons in the different yugas religious, appropriate to those yugas.

यासना कारणं राजनसर्विकं धर्मवैस्थिति ।

तस्यां वे मतिनयां तु धर्मोषि मतिनो घबेत् ॥ ८ ॥

O king! In all cases where the conservation of religions and religious affairs are concerned, know that the original wish and desire is the cause. If this desire be impure and sullied, religion becomes also sullied; for, verily, this impurity in one's desire is one's cause of ruin in every respect. (Therefore the impure desires are never to be cherished and indulged.)

मतिना वासना सर्व विनाश्योति सर्वथा ।

बहाणणे हदयाज्ञातः पुत्रो धर्म इति स्मृतः ॥ 9 ॥

A son, named Dharma, was born of the heart of Brahmā; he was devoted to Brahmajñāna (the knowledge of Brahma), truthful, and always engaged in rites and ceremonies and in accordance with the Vedic religion.

बहाणण: सत्यस्म्यजो वेदधार्मतः सदा ।

दक्षस्य दुहिताएं हिं घृता द्रग महात्मना ॥ 10 ॥

This high souled Muni Dharma was a householder and married duly, according to the proper procedure, to the ten daughters of Dakṣa Prajāpati.

विवहाविधिता सम्यकः मुनिना गृहधारिण।

तास्वजीजनयतुज्ञान्यं: सत्यवर्ता बर: ॥ 11 1 ॥

This Dharma, the foremost amongst the followers of truth, impregnated them and had four sons, named respectively Hari, Kṛṣṇa, Nara, and Nārāyaṇa.

हरिं कृष्ण नरं चैदा तथा नारायणं नृप ।

योगाध्यासराते निवर्ध दीर: कृष्णो बभूव हु ॥ १२ ॥

Hari and Kṛṣṇa, amongst the four, used to remain always in the practising of the yoga.

नस्नारायणी चैव चेसुत्सप उतनम् ।

प्रातेल्यार्टि समायान तीर्थे बदरीकाभ्ये ॥ १३ ॥

Nara and Nārāyana came over to the Himālayan mountains and, in the hermitage of Badarikā, commenced the difficult religious asceticism and penance.

तपविस्तु ध्रुर्णी ती पुराणी भुनिसन्ती ।

गृणांती तस्यं बढ़ा गंगाया विपुले तदे ॥ 14 ॥

The foremost of the ascetics, those two ancient Munis, began to recite that highest mantra of Para Brahma, the Gāyatrī, on the wide spacious bank of the Ganges.

हरेंडेशी स्थिती तत्र ननतारायणावृषी ।

पूर्णं वर्षसहसं तु चक्राते तप उतमम् ॥ 15 ॥

The two Rṣis named Nara and Nārāyaṇa, born of Hari's Anīsa, practised excellent tapasyā for full one thousand years.

Book IV Chapter V

तापितं च जगत्सर्वे तपसा सचाराचरम् ।

नरनारायणायचं च शक्र: क्षीभं तदा ययौ ॥ १ ६ ॥

The whole Universe, moving and unmoving, became hot through the Fire of their Tapas. Indra became also perplexed.

चिन्ताविहः सहस्वाक्षो मनसा समकल्ययत् ।

कि कत्वं धधपुत्रो तापसी ध्यानसंयुत् ॥ १७ ॥

सिद्धार्थी सुयुणं श्रेष्ठमानं नो ग्रहीधानः ।

विज्ञ: कर्थ प्रकर्तैल्यतपो येन भवेत्र हि ॥ 18 ॥

The thousandeyed Indra became anxious, and thought within himself thus: What is to be done now? These two sons of Dharma are practising Tapas and are in meditation. If they succeed, they can occupy my excellent seat in Heaven; how can I break their Tapasyā and what steps shall I take to hinder them.

उत्पाद कामं क्रोधं च लोभे जयातिर्दारभम्।

इत्थिद्रियसहख्रशः समाकृह गजोतनम् ॥ १९ ॥

विधन्कामस्तु तत्सा जगाम गन्धमादनम् ।

गत्वा तजामे पुण्ये तावयश्यच्छतकुः ॥ 20 ॥

Lust, anger, and insurmountable avarice Indra brought into existence and, intent on hindering their tapasyā, mounted on the elephant Airāvata, went quickly to the hill Gandhamādana, and approaching the holy hermitage, saw the two ancient Rsis.

तस्मा दीनदोहै तु भात्मकाविव कोदिति ।

ब्रहाविष्यु किमेती वे प्रकटो व विभावसु ॥ २ १ ॥

धार्पुरावृष्टि तृती तस्या किं कारित्यः ।

Their bodies were incandescent by Tapasyā, as if they were the two rising Suns. Were they Brahmā, Viṣṇu manifested there or were they the two shining sources of light? These two Rsis were the sons of Dharma. What would they do with their Tapasyā?

इति संघित्य ती दूद्या तृतीवाच श्रवीपिति: ॥ 22 ॥

किं वां कार्य महागभी कृतं धर्मसुती किल ।

ददामि वां वरं श्रेष्ठ दातुं यातोउम्यदूमपी ॥ 23 ॥

अंदयमायि दास्यामि तुहोउम्मि तपसा किल ।

Thinking thus, the lord of Śacī seeing them addressed thus: “O highly fortunate ones! O two Rṣis the sons of Dharma! Please tell me what are your objects? I have come here to give thee excellent boons; I am very pleased with your Tapasyā; therefore ask boons of me; and even if they be not worth-giving, I will give them to you.

व्यास उवाच

एवं पुन: पुनः शक्रस्तावुवाय पुन: स्थितः ॥ 24 ॥

नोचृतनाडुर्षी ट्यानसंसिंधनों दृढ़वेतसौ ।

ततो वे मोहिनी मायां चकार भयदां वृषः ॥ 25 ॥

Vyāsa said: The Rsis were deeply immersed in meditation and seemed very firm and resolute; they, therefore, did not reply anything, though Indra, standing before them, repeatedly urged them to ask boons from him. Seeing this, the king of the Immortals began to terrify them with his supernatural enchanting fearful māyic powers.

युकान्सित्रांश व्याखांश सुस्यावाश्रविभीयम् ।

वर्षे वातं तथा वाईंस सुमुभावा चुन: पुनः ॥ 26 ॥

He created lions, tigers, wolves and other murderous animals and began to terrify the two Rsis with them; Indra also produced rains, hurricanes and fires very frequently so that they might yield.

भीष्यामास तौ शक्रो मायांं कृत्वा विमोहित्रीम् ।

भयतो2पि वर्ष नीती न तो धर्मेतुती मुनी ॥ 27 ॥

नरनारायणी दृष्टव शक्र स्वभवं नगः ।

In spite of Indra's attempt to terrify them by his wonderful Māyā, the two Munis, Nara Nārāyaṇa, the two sons of Dharma, could not be brought under his control. And Indra returned to his own place.

बरदाने प्रतुळ्ही न न भीति वहिवायुतः ॥ 28 ॥

व्याप्सिंहादिपि: क्रान्ती चलिता नाश्रमात्सक्त।।

न तत्योधानिभां वे कृतं कोर्पि क्षमोअपवत् ॥ 29 ॥

इन्होपि सदनं गत्वा चिंतयामास दु:खितः ।

चलिता भवलोगमथां नेगो मुनिवरोगमे ॥ 30 ॥

चिन्तपनो महाविद्यामादिशानिकं स्मातनीम् ।

ईशर्ती संलोकानां पर्ग प्रकृतिमद्धुतान् ॥ 31 ॥

ध्यातचत कः क्षमो लोके बहुभगवादिवदुतः ।

And he became very sorry and thought thus:

"These two Munis could not be tempted away with boons, nor did they fly away from their place of worship, though terrified with fire, wind, wolves, tigers and lions. No one, I think, would be able to break their meditai on. When fear and temptations have not distracted their meditation, they are certainly meditating on the Eternal Mahā Vidyā Śri Bhuvanesvari, the Prime Force of Nature, the Source of all Māyās, and the Goddess the Creatrix of all the worlds, the wonderful highest Prakrti; what other expert in creating Māyā there can be? Who can break their meditation!

यमूल्ना: सकल्पा माया देवासुरुक्ताः किन्ति ॥ ३२ ॥

ते कंबं वाधितुं शक्ता ध्यायिते गतकल्यपाः ।

Indeed! how can this whole host of Māyā that are created by Gods and Asuras overpower those purged of all their sins, who are meditating their Creator, the Supreme Māyā, that Illusion by which one considers the unreal Universe as really existent and as distinct from the Supreme Spirit, whence the Gods and Asuras have derived all their supernatural powers.

वार्थकी कामकीवं च मायाकीं तथैव च ॥ ३३ ॥

त्रिते यस्य भवेते नु वायितुं कोर्पि न क्षमः।

He in whose heart reign the seed mantras of Vāk, Kāma and Māyā, (called Vāgbjām, Kāmabjām, Māyābjām, no one is able to stand against and overpower him.

मायया मोहितः शक्रो भूयस्तस्य प्रतिक्रियम् ॥ 34 ॥

कर्तुं कामवस्तौ तु समातुयावबीद्धचः ।

मनोभववसंतेन रत्वा युक्तो बजाउनु ॥ 35 ॥

अभ्वतोषिः: समायुक्तस्तसा गन्धभावन् ।

O king! Indra, enchanted by Māyā, did not desist from tempting the two Rṣis, but he went on thinking other means by which their asceticism could be baffled and asked Kāma and Vasantā (the god of Lust and the srpiug season) to come before him and addressed them, thus: "O Kāma! You now be united with your wife Rati and Vasantā (the God of spring) and go to the hill Gandhamādaṇa,

accompanied by all the Apsarās (celestial damsels) and with all the Rasas (love sentiments).

Note: The Gandhamādaṇa is mountain like unsurpassable intoxicating happiness of the senses.

नरनावायणी तत्र पुराणाप्रियस्तनो Ⅱ 36 Ⅱ

कुरुस्तत्प एकान्ते स्थिती बद्रिकाओंमे ।

गत्वा तत्र सर्वीये तु तयोर्नम्ब्व मार्गेणि: Ⅱ 37 Ⅱ

वित्तं कामातुंरं कार्यं कुरु कार्यं मायामुंना ।

There you will find the two ancient excellent Rsis Nara and Nārāyana practising asceticism in solitude, in the hermitage of Badarikā. O Manamatha! You better go before them, and with the influence of your arrows, do now my work and make hearts extremely lustful.

मोह्यित्योच्चार्यित्या विजिगढ़ेताउवायु च ॥ 38 ॥

वशीकृक महाभाग मुनी धर्मेतुरावि ।

O Furtunate One! Charm over them by means of your arrows, make them leave their asceticism by magical spells.

को ह्यास्मिनसंसंसारे देवो दैत्योज्य मानवः ॥ 39 ॥

यस्ते बाणवर्ग प्राप्तो न याति भूगतादितः ।

Who is there in this world of Devas, Daityas, human beings, that, being whipped by your arrows, do not come under your control?

ब्रह्मार्ति गिरितानावश्छंत्रे बहिंदियमितिः ॥ ४० ॥

गणना कॉन्वोः काम त्वबुणानं पराकमे ।

When Brahmā, I, Mahādeva, Moon and Fire are all fascinated by your arrows, then is there any doubt that these two Rṣis would not be fascinated by them!

वारांगनागणोर्धं ते सहायार्थं मवेरितः ॥ 4 1 ॥

आगमिष्यति तत्रैव रभ्यादीनां मनोरमः ।

I am sending these public women as your assistants. Rambhā and other beautiful celestial nymphs would all follow you.

एका तितोतमा रभ्या कार्ये साधायिं श्रम ॥ 42 ॥

त्यमेवैकः श्रमः कामं चित्रितैः कर्तु संशयः।

You alone, or Rambhā or Tilottamā alone can do this work. Will there be any doubt if you all unite in this?

Book IV Chapter VI

कुरु कार्य महाभाग ददामि तव वार्षिछतम् ॥ 4 3 ॥

O Good One! Do this work for me; I will confer on you your desired objects.

प्रलोभिती मवास्तवर्ध वरदानैस्तपस्थिति ।

स्थानात्र चलिती शांतीं वृचायउं मे ततः श्रभः ॥ 4 4 ॥

O Manmatha! I tempted them with boons but these two ascetics, of controlled minds, could not be displaced from their seat. My efforts were rendered useless.

तथा वे भायया कृत्वा भीषिति तापसौ भूणम् ।

तथापि नोत्थिती स्थानाहेहरक्षापरे न तो ॥ 4 5 ॥

I frightened them very much with all the Māyic powers; yet they could not be dislocated from their deep thoughts. It seems that they are quite heedless in the preservation of their bodies.

व्यास उवाच

इति तस्य वचः श्रुत्वा जाक्र प्राप्त मानोषवः ।

वासवादु कार्यवामि कार्य ते मनसोषितम् ॥ 46 ॥

Vyāsa said: Kāmadeva, on hearing the king of the Devas, addressed him thus: "O Indra! To day I will fulfill all your desires.

यदि विज्युं महेशं वा ब्रह्माणं वा दिवाकमम् ।

ध्यार्वती तौ तद्धउसाकं भवित्तीरा वर्गी मुनी ॥ 47 ॥

But there is one word. If these two ascetics be meditating Viṣṇu, Śiva or Brahmā or the Sun, then I will bring them under my control.

देवीभर्ते वर्गीकर्तूं नाहं शरकः कर्णचन ।

कामारां महावीजं चिन्तयन्तं मनस्यलम् ॥ 48 ॥

And if they be meditating on the Great Seed Mantra, the root of all Māyā, and the great Kāmabījam, the king of the Kāma, I will never be able to subdue such a devotee of the Highest Devi. तां देवीं चेम्हागर्जिति संश्रिती भक्तिभावतः ।

न तदा मम बाणानां गोचरी ताप्पसे किकल ॥ ४ ९ ॥

If these two ascetics have devotedly taken refuge of the Great Power Mahā Devi, then they will not come under the sight of my arrows."

इन्द्र उवाच

गच्छ त्वं च महाभाग सर्वस्तत्र समुभृतेः ।

कार्य मापातितुःसाध्ये कर्ता हितमनुतमम् ॥ 50 ॥

Indra said: “O Blessed One! Go now with your assistants, ready to do your work. Nobody but you, I find, that can fulfill my this beneficial, though very difficult work.”

व्यास उवाच

इति तेन समाधिदा ययु: सर्वे मयुधाताः ।

यत्र ती धर्मपुत्री द्वै तेपाते उप्करं तपः ॥ 51 ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणो चतुर्भिकल्भे

पत्रमो5याय || 15 || 1

Vyāsa said: Thus ordered by Indra, they all departed to where the Dharma's sons Nara, and Nārāyana were performing their hard Tapasyās.

Here ends the Filth Chapter in the Fourth Book of

Śrimadkevibhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam of

18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book IV Chapter VI

CHAPTER VI

On the Origin of Urvási

व्यास उवाच

प्रथमं तत्र सम्प्राटो वसन्तः पर्वतींत्तमे ।

पुधिताः पादपाः सर्वे डिंरेभालिर्वारिजिताः ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! First there appeared, on the mountain, the king of the seasons, Vasanta, the Spring. All the trees flowered and became very beautiful; and the bees began to hum round all sides.

आमाश्र डकुला रथासितलका: किंशुका: शृभा: ।

सालालासालास्मालाश मधुका: पुचिता बधु: ॥ 2 ॥

Mangoes, Bokulla trees, the beautiful Tilaka trees, the good Kiriśukas, Sāla, Tāla, Tamāla and Madhuka trees assumed unequalled beauties, ornamented with their flowers.

बधूवुः कोकिलालापा वृक्षायेषु मनोहराः ।

वल्ल्योदपि पुधिताः स्वां आलिलिंनुनीगोतमान् ॥ 3 ॥

Cuckoos began to coo coo (warble) beautiful on the tops of trees; the creepers flowered and began to embrace the trees.

प्राणिनः ख्वायु भार्यातु प्रेमयुक्ता: स्मातुतः ।

बभुव्छातिनात्रा क्रीडासक्ताः परस्वम् ॥ 4 ॥

The creatures became enamoured with love and began to look on their paramours with amorous eyes and began to hold pleasant sexual intercourses.

वसुर्भूत: सुगन्धाशु सुस्पर्शा दक्षिणानिताः ।

इन्द्रियाणि प्रमाहीति मुन्नौतामपि चाघवत् ॥ 5 ॥

The southern breeze blew gently, full of pleasant odours and agreeable to touch. The sensual organs became very powerful and could no longer be brought under their control by the Munis.

तित्युक्ततः कामः पूर्यन्यघ्न मारणान् ।

चका चरितस्तत्र वासं बदोरिकाओं ॥ 6 ॥

Then Kāma, united with Rati, hurriedly entered into the Hermitage of Badarikā with the five arrows in his hands.

रभा तिलोनमाद्धाङ्ग गत्वा तत्र वाराङ्गे ।

गानं चकुः सुगीतज्ञः स्वतातनसम्वित्यम् ॥ 7 ॥

Rambhā, Tilottamā, and other prominent Apsarās all went to that beautiful! hermitage and began to sing in perfect tune with gamuts, key notes and respective pauses.

तच्छ्वा मधुरोद्रौं कौकिलानां च कुजितम् ।

भ्रमतिलिवारं च प्रबुद्धौ तो मुनीधरी ॥ ८ ॥

The two Mahariss awoke on hearing the sweet music, the warblings of the cuckoos and the nice hummings of the bees.

ऋतुराजमकले तु दूद्या तो पुषितं नन् ।

जाती चिन्तापरो तत्र ननतारयाणची ॥ 9 ॥

Nara Nārāyaṇa became anxious to see the untimely bursting of the Vasanta (vernal season) and the flowering of the trees.

किमय शिशिरायाय: संवृत: समयं विना ।

प्राणिजी विद्वाल: सर्वे लक्ष्मोपेत्तिमातृतः ॥ 10 ॥

How can the spring season come now at such an untimely season. I see, all the creatures are

become extremely amorous with each other and infatuated with passionate lust.

कालधर्मविपर्यासः कथमघ दुर्गासदः ।

नरं नारायण: प्राह विस्यमोतुल्ललोचनः ॥ १ १ ॥

It is very unusual that untimely things should happen. How has this come to pass? Stuck with wonder, Nārāyaṇa began to speck to Nara with eyes wide apart.

नारायण उवाच

पथ्य ध्रातरिये वृक्षा: पुथिता: प्रतिभान्ति वे ।

कोकिलालायसंयुहा ध्रमारतिविजिताः ॥ 12 ॥

Nārāyaṇa said: “O Brother! See these trees look very elegant with flowers on them; the cuckoos are sounding sweet notes on all sides; the bees are humming on all sides.

शिशिरं भीमातर्नां दारायन्वखर्नैवंः ।

वसंतैक्सरी प्राप्त: पलाशकुर्मपैनुं ॥ 13 ॥

The spring, the lion of the seasons, has burst asunder the fierce elephant, the winter season, by its sharp nails, as testified by the budding of Palāsa flowers.

राकाणीककात तन्वी देवर्ष किंशुकांधिका ।

नीलाणोककाना श्यामा विवाकीसकमलाना ॥ 14 ॥

नीलैंदीवतेना सा विज्वबुङ्घफलती ।

प्रोतुल्लोकुंदरदाना मछरी कर्णशीभता ॥ 15 ॥

पंधुजीक्षाया गृधा सिन्धुवारणखोद्वा ।

पुंकाकिलित्वसा पुणया कद्ववद्वसना सुभा ॥ 16 ॥

बाईवृन्दकालाप च सासस्वन्नपुरा ।

वासन्तो बढ़राना भनहंभगतिसथा ॥ 17 ॥

पुत्रजीवांशुक्यस्त रेमार्जिनिर्वारिता ।

वसन्तक्षीः सम्पाता बह्ञाव्यदीराक्रप्रे ॥ 18 ॥

O Brāhmīn! See how beautiful and excellent has become this hermitage with the presence of the Goddess Spring Lakṣmī? O Devarsī! The Raktāśoka flower is the palm of her hand; Kiniśuka flowers, her excellent feet; Nilāśoka flowers, her black hair on her head, the full-blown lotuses, her eyes; the bel fruits, her breast; the jolly Kunda flowers, her teeth; Mañjari, her beautiful ears; red Bandhu flowers, her lips; Sindhuvāra, her wonderful nails;

Book IV Chapter VI

the peacocks, her ornaments; the sounds of Sārasa birds, the jingling of her feet ornaments; the wreathes of flowers, her waist ornaments; the mad gooses, her gait; Kadamba flower's filaments, her hairs on her body; O best of ascetics! With all these, the Vasanta Lakṣmī has assumed a wonderful nice appearance.

अकाले किमियं प्राता विस्मयोउयं ममाधुना ।

तपोविफकारा नूनं देवर्षे परिधिंतय न 119 ॥

Why has this occurred untimely? Think over it; O Devarṣi! I am struck with wonder; surely this is obstructive of our penances.

भूयते सुरानीर्णां गानं ध्यानविनारानम् ।

आवयोस्तपिभंगाय कृतं मघवता किल ॥ 20 ॥

Hear! There the Apsarās are singing sweetly the song, tending to destroy our Tapasyās; it seems, these are the means, no doubt, adopted by Indra to pollute our Tapasyā.

མཆུང་བྱུམ་གྱུང་གྱུང་གྱུང་གྱུང་གྱུང་གྱུང་གྱུང་གྱུ

विनोद्यं विहितो भाति भीतेनासुराज्युण ॥ 21 ॥

Why is this spring season now generating our pleasures? It is clear that that. Indra, the enemy of the Asuras, is become afraid of our Tapasyā and is creating these obstructions to disturb our asceticisms.

वाता: सुगन्धा: शीला प्रसामाति मनोहा: ।

नान्यकारणमस्तीह शतक्रतुर्कर्ति बिना ॥ 22 ॥

Lo! The cool, odorous, and pleasant breezes is blowing; no other cause can be traced than the wicked deed of Indra."

इति बुवति विप्राप्ये देने नारायणे विभो ।

सर्वे द्विष्यं प्राता मन्यप्रमुखास्तात ॥ 23 ॥

When the best of the Brāhmins, the Deva Nārāyaṇa was addressing thus, the whole host of Cupid became visible before their sight.

दूरर्गं भगवान्यवर्त्रजे नारायणतशा ।

वियमायाश्यमनसी बभूवतुरभावपि ॥ 24 ॥

And the two Rsis were very much surprised on seeing them.

मान्यं मेकां दीव रभां दैव लिलोतमाम् ।

पुष्पान्यां सुकेशीं च महाश्रितां मारोपायम् ॥ 25 ॥

प्रमूत्रां घृताङ्गी व गीतजां चासहासिनीम् ।

चन्द्रप्रभां च सोमां च कोकिलालापमधिद्धतम् ॥ 26 ॥

विद्युभालांबुजाईं च तथा काङ्गनामिनीम् ।

एताङ्गान्या वरात्रोहा घृतसाध्यां ततदान्तिके ॥ 27 ।

They saw near to them the Cupid with his attendants Menakā, Rambhā, Tilottamā, Puspagandhā, Sukesī, Mahāśvetā, Manoranmā, Pramodvarā, Ghrṭāci, Čaruhāsini, the expert in music, Candra Prabhā, the cuckoo voiced Somā, the lotus eyed Vidyunmālā, Kāncanamlinī, and others.

तासां ऋषसहस्वाणि पञ्ञारद्धिकानि च।

वीक्ष्य ती विक्सितो जाती कामकैस्यं सुविसतम् ॥ 28 ॥

Eight thousand and five hundred Apsarās and long multitudes of the hosts of Cupid, the Munis saw and were surprised.

प्रणम्याश्रे स्थिताः सर्वां देववारांगनास्ततः ।

दिव्याभरणभूमात्वा दिव्यान्योपोषभितः ॥ 29 ॥

Then those prostitutes of the Devas, dressed with their heavenly ornaments and the heavenly flowers, appeared before the Munis and bowed down their heads on the ground.

जगुष्ठलेन ताः सर्वा: पृथिव्यामितुलर्भम् ।

ततचावस्वित्यां दिव्यां मन्यथातिविवधान् ॥ 30 ॥

The Apsarās began their enchanting songs, exciting much passion and rarely heard or seen in this world.

शुषाव भगवान्विणुर्नो नारायणस्ततः ।

शुत्वा प्रोवाच सातस्त प्रीत्या नारायणो मुनि: ॥ 31 ॥

आस्वतां सुखमदैव करोमातिलयमद्धूनम् ।

भवन्योर्जितिध्यॉण प्रापतः स्वर्गाल्यमध्यम: ॥ 32 ॥

The two Munis Bhagavān Viṣṇu like Nara Nārāyaṇa were pleased with their music and addressed them thus: O thin waisted good looking Apsarās! You have come here as guests, I see, from your Heavenly world. Stay here in peace and all comfort; we will gladly serve you as your hosts."

व्यास उवाच

साधिभानस्तु संजातस्तवा नारायणो मुनि: । इन्द्रैण प्रेयिता नूनं तथा विद्यन्धिकरीयां ॥ ३ ३ ॥

वरायकः का इमा: सर्वी: सुजाम्यद्ध नावः किल ।

तृणभ्यो दिव्यस्पाधा दर्यायिम तपोबलम् ॥ 34 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! The two Munis, thinking that Indra has sent these Apsarās to obstruct their Tapasyā, were filled with egoism and determined to create, out of their strength of Tapasyā a new Apsarā, who would be very much more beautiful and possessing far more heavenly graces than the present ones, who are ordinary looking and clumsy in their behaviours.

इति संघिन्य मनसा करोगोंः प्रताङ्य चै ।

तस्सोत्पादयामास नारीं सर्वांकुषुन्दीम् ॥ 35 ॥

And the Munis, by clapping or striking their thighs, instantly created a woman, exquisitely beautiful in all respects.

नारायणोरभ्यमृत हुवैर्गिति ततः शुभा ।

दूरशुता: स्थितास्तात्र सियमयं परयं युः ॥ 36 ॥

This good looking woman was named Urvaśī, since she was produced from the thighs. And all the other Apsarās present there were very much thunderstruck on seeing that Urvaśī.

तासां च परिचार्यां तात्तीशातिसुन्दरी: ।

प्रादुशकार तसा तदा युनिसंम्बभ: ॥ 37 ॥

Then Muni Nārāyana easily created as many women as there were Apsarās to serve them.

गायल्थ इंसल्थ नानोपायनपाणव: ।

प्रमेमुस्ता मुनी सर्वा: स्थिता: कृत्वोउलिं पुरः ॥ 38 ॥

The just produced Apsarās brought with them all sorts of offerings in their hands, and, singing and smiling, came before the Munis and with clasped hands bowed down before them.

तां वीक्ष्य विभ्रमकर्ती तपसो विभूर्ति

देवांगना हि मुगुड़: प्रविमोहरंचल: ।

क्रत्रुधु ती प्रमुदिताननपश्ठोगेभा

रोगोहमालेलसितधान्तिनाजंगलल्पः ॥ ३ ९ ॥

The heavenly damsels sent by Indra, though enchanting to others, were themselves now bewildered on beholding Urvaśī, beautiful in all respects and produced out of the Tapasyā of the Munis; and their hairs over the bodies stood on

their ends. Then they tried to make their faces as beautiful as they could and began to address the Munis thus:

कुरुः कथं स्तुतिमहो तपसी महन्चं

धैर्य तर्वेय भवतामिधीक्ष्वेय बाला: ।

अस्मत्कारक्षियविद्याश्रोण द्धधः

को चा न ततभवातो मनसो व्यथा नः ॥ 40 ॥

O Munis! We are ignorant girls; how can we praise you and the greatness of your Tapasyā and at your steadiness. Oh!
